       69 +> updated once it has been verified.                          
       70 +                                                              
       71 +## Model configuration (safe)                                 
       72 +                                                              
       73 +Configuration is read from environment variables, loaded from 
          +a local `.env` file that is                                   
       74 +**git-ignored and never committed**. Only the template `.env.e
          +xample` is in the                                             
       75 +repository. Keys are never printed, logged or written to repor
          +ts; the UI only shows                                         
       76 +whether a model is configured.                                
       77 +                                                              
       78 +```bash                                                       
       79 +cp .env.example .env      # then edit .env and add your key(s)
       80 +```                                                           
       81 +                                                              
       82 +| Variable | Purpose | Default in `.env.example` |            
       83 +|---|---|---|                                                 
       84 +| `LLM_PROVIDER` | `groq`, `gemini`, `cerebras`, `mistral`, `o
          +llama` or `openai_compatible` | `groq` |                      
       85 +| `LLM_MODEL` | Model ID at that provider | `qwen/qwen3.8-27b`
          + |                                                            
       86 +| `LLM_BASE_URL` | Optional endpoint override (required for `o
          +penai_compatible`) | empty |                                  
       87 +| `LLM_API_KEY` | Optional; if empty, the provider's own varia
          +ble is used | empty |                                         
       88 +| `GROQ_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, … | Provider keys | empty 
          +|                                                             
       89 +| `LLM_FALLBACK_PROVIDER`, `LLM_FALLBACK_MODEL` | Optional sec
          +ond model, used once if the first fails (rate limit, outage) |
          + `gemini`, `gemini-3.5-flash` |                               
       90 +| `LLM_TIMEOUT_S`, `LLM_MAX_TOKENS` | Optional tuning | `600`,
          + `16384` |                                                    
       91 +                                                              
       92 +Free keys: Groq at https://console.groq.com/keys, Google AI St
          +udio at                                                       
       93 +https://aistudio.google.com/apikey. A local model also works: 
       94 +`ollama pull qwen3:8b`, then `LLM_PROVIDER=ollama`, `LLM_MODEL
          +=qwen3:8b` and                                                
       95 +`LLM_TIMEOUT_S=1800` (much slower and less accurate, see the e
          +valuation).                                                   
       96 +                                                              
       97 +**Why this model:** five models on four providers were compare
          +d on a task-specific dataset                                  
       98 +(7 change scenarios × 12 tests = 84 hand-labelled judgments). 
          +Groq `qwen/qwen3.8-27b` had                                   
       99 +the best critical-case score and fully consistent answers, and
          + takes about 7 s per                                          
      100 +analysis. The method, results and limitations are in          
      101 +[docs/llm_evaluation.md](docs/llm_evaluation.md).             
      102 +                                                              
      103 +## What is live and what is replayed                          
      104 +                                                              
      105 +Every result in the UI and in the report states its source:   
      106 +                                                              
      107 +| Label | Meaning |                                           
      108 +|---|---|                                                     
      109 +| **Live** | The configured model was called now, for exactly 
          +these inputs. |                                               
      110 +| **Replayed** | Real output of `groq:qwen/qwen3.8-27b`, recor
          +ded on 2026-11-16 during the evaluation and committed in [`dat
          +a/replay/`](data/replay/). No model is called. |              
      111 +| **Cached** | An earlier live run in this environment (`.cach
          +e/`, git-ignored). |                                          
      112 +| **Not analysed** | No recording exists and no model is confi
          +gured or available. The diff and the parsed register facts are
          + still shown; no assessment is invented. |                    
      113 +                                                              
      114 +Recordings exist for the provided files (the real revision B) 
          +and for the six evaluation                                    
      115 +scenarios in [`eval/scenarios/`](eval/scenarios/) (upload one 
          +as the revised spec to see                                    
      116 +it). Any other edit needs a configured model. Recordings are m
          +atched on a hash of the                                       
      117 +exact model input (prompt version, prompt text, parsed changes
          + and register), so a                                          
      118 +recording is never shown for inputs it was not made for. Deter
          +ministic post-checks are                                      
      119 +re-applied to recordings when they are loaded.                
      120 +                                                              
      121 +## How to reproduce a result                                  
      122 +                                                              
      123 +```bash                                                       
      124 +# The provided files, from the recorded analysis (no key):    
      125 +uv run vcu-impact analyze --mode replay                       
      126 +# Same, and regenerate the committed example report:          
      127 +make report                                                   
      128 +# A change of your own, with a live model (needs a key in .env
          +):                                                            
      129 +uv run vcu-impact analyze --revised path/to/your_spec.docx --m
          +ode live --report out/report.html                             
      130 +# The LLM evaluation tables, from the stored runs:            
      131 +make eval-score                                               
      132 +```                                                           
      133 +                                                              
      134 +In the UI: open **3 · Edit and re-analyse**, change for exampl
          +e `within 80 milliseconds`                                    
      135 +to `within 90 milliseconds` in VCU-DR-01 and apply. Tab 1 show
          +s the new diff; then click                                    
      136 +**Run analysis with the LLM** in the sidebar. **4 · Report** d
          +ownloads the result.                                          
      137 +                                                              
      138 +## How it works                                               
      139 +                                                              
      140 +```                                                           
      141 +baseline.docx ─┐                                   ┌─ replay: 
          +recorded run                                                  
      142 +revised.docx ──┼─ parse ─► diff ─► LLM input ─► key ┼─ live: L
          +LM (strict JSON schema) ─► post-checks                        
      143 +register.xlsx ─┘  (code)   (code)                   └─ none: f
          +acts only                                                     
      144 +                                       │                      
      145 +                         per-test rows ─► UI tabs + HTML/Markd
          +own report                                                    
      146 +```                                                           
      147 +                                                              
      148 +| Done by code (deterministic) | Done by the LLM (judgment) | 
      149 +|---|---|                                                     
      150 +| Parse requirements by ID pattern; diff requirements, titles,
          + numbers and other sections | Interpret each change (tightened
          +, relaxed, reworded, …) and what it means for verification |  
      151 +| Parse the register; classify each link as valid, missing, TB
          +D, unknown or removed | Judge every test's relevance, **includ
          +ing tests with no valid link** |                              
      152 +| Extract values from free-text results (e.g. `PASS (94 ms)` →
          + 94 ms) | Compare evidence with the new requirement: same quan
          +tity, same clock (workshop notes) |                           
      153 +| Set `fix_link` for every broken link | Decide re-run vs proc
          +edure update vs owner decision; write the reason and evidence 
          +|                                                             
      154 +| Validate the output: all tests covered, no invented IDs, no 
          +approval wording, contradiction checks | |                    
      155 +                                                              
      156 +The LLM output is a strict JSON schema validated with Pydantic
          +, with one automatic                                          
      157 +repair retry. The UI and the report are built only from valida
          +ted data.                                                     
      158 +                                                              
      159 +## Assumptions                                                
      160 +                                                              
      161 +- **Specification format:** a requirement starts with a short 
          +paragraph beginning with an                                   
      162 +  ID such as `VCU-DR-01` (upper-case parts separated by `-`, e
          +nding in a number),                                           
      163 +  optionally followed by a title. Its text runs until the next
          + requirement or heading.                                      
      164 +  Styling is not used, so files re-saved in Word still parse. 
          +Duplicate IDs are rejected.                                   
      165 +- **The baseline is the reference.** Edits made in the UI appl
          +y to the revised                                              
      166 +  specification. Requirements are matched by ID; a renamed ID 
          +counts as removed plus added.                                 
      167 +- **Register format:** the header row is found by column names
          + (`Test case`,                                                
      168 +  `Req reference`, `Result`, …) in the first 20 rows, so title
          + rows above it are fine. The                                  
      169 +  workshop notes are read from any sheet with `Date` and `Obse
          +rvation` columns. Links may                                   
      170 +  list several IDs separated by `;`, `,` or `/`.              
      171 +- **Broken links:** an empty cell, `TBD`/`TBC`/`N/A`, an ID fo
          +und in neither                                                
      172 +  specification (e.g. `VCU-OLD-88`) or an ID removed in the re
          +vision are broken links:                                      
      173 +  they always get `fix_link`, and are never taken as proof tha
          +t a test is unrelated.                                        
      174 +- **Evidence, not approval:** a previous `PASS` is evidence fr
          +om an earlier campaign that                                   
      175 +  may not cover a newer requirement. Reports never state appro
          +val; a post-check flags                                       
      176 +  approval-like wording.                                      
      177 +- **Workshop notes are context for the LLM**, not hard-coded r
          +ules (e.g. brake and torque                                   
      178 +  latency are measured on different clocks).                  
      179 +- **Only requirement changes trigger an assessment.** Other ch
          +anged sections (e.g.                                          
      180 +  revision notes) are shown and passed to the model as context
          +. If no requirement                                           
      181 +  changed, no model is called.                                
      182 +- **Scale:** evaluated on a 12-test register. The UI warns abo
          +ve 60 tests; larger                                           
      183 +  registers would need batching (not implemented).            
      184 +- **Single local user:** no authentication, no database; state
          + lives in the browser                                         
      185 +  session, live results are cached in `.cache/`. The register 
          +can be replaced by upload                                     
      186 +  but not edited in the app.                                  
      187 +- **Fictional data only.** Recorded analyses and reports conta
          +in only the provided                                          
      188 +  fictional material.                                         
      189 +                                                              
      190 +## Known issues and limitations                               
      191 +                                                              
      192 +See [docs/known_issues.md](docs/known_issues.md). The most imp
          +ortant one: a **pure                                          
      193 +rewording** of a requirement can be reported as *directly affe
          +cted* although the model                                      
      194 +itself says the behaviour is unchanged. The application detect
          +s this contradiction and                                      
      195 +shows a warning next to the test; it does not override the mod
          +el's status.                                                  
      196 +                                                              
      197 +The evaluation is small (84 judgments, one register, one annot
          +ator), and the prompt was                                     
      198 +refined on the same scenarios. Free-tier latency, rate limits 
          +and availability can change                                   
      199 +without notice.

❯ Something I did not understand: how can the llm flag S5 TV-005 automatically?
  it's flagged because we knew forehand that the llm was wrong or is there

● It's automatic detection, not something hard-coded for TV-005. I'll check that
  claim against all the stored runs before explaining, so I'm not just
  asserting it:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● The rule is generic. It doesn't know about TV-005, S5 or the DGN-04
  requirement. It fired on exactly one test across all 7 recorded scenarios, and
  stayed silent on the other six scenarios.

  How it works

  The model returns two things in one answer:
  1. Its reading of each changed requirement, a nature field such as tightened,
     relaxed or reworded_same_meaning.
  2. A status for each test, plus the changed requirements that status relates
     to.

  The check in analysis.post_check compares the two. If a test is
  directly_affected and every requirement it relates to was read as
  reworded_same_meaning, the model has contradicted itself. A rewording changes
  no behaviour, so no test can be directly affected by it. The code adds a
  warning to that test.

  In S5 the model read DGN-04 as reworded_same_meaning and wrote in TV-005's
  reason that "the behavior is unchanged". It still marked TV-005
  directly_affected, so the rule fires. The same rule would fire for any other
  test or requirement that ends up in that situation.

  The part that is hindsight

  We didn't know in advance that the model was wrong about TV-005. We found the
  failure during the evaluation, and that is why I wrote this rule. So the rule
  is general in form, but I chose it because of a failure I had already seen.

  What it can't do

  It catches only one kind of error: the model disagreeing with itself in a
  specific way. It does not know whether the model is right. It would miss:
  - a model that misreads a real change as a rewording, or a rewording as a real
    change, and is consistent about it;
  - a test given related_requirements: [], because the rule needs a related
    requirement to look up;
  - any wrong status that doesn't contradict the model's own interpretation. For
    example, TV-002 could be wrong without anything flagging it.

  I've only seen it fire on this one case, and the ones it stayed silent on are
  the six scenarios above. Six silent scenarios show it doesn't raise false
  alarms there, not that it would catch other mistakes. The documentation says
  so too: docs/known_issues.md describes it as "makes the contradiction
  visible", not as a fix.

  If you want it to do more

  - Downgrade automatically. Today it only warns and leaves the model's status
    alone. I kept it that way so that a disagreement is visible rather than
    silently overridden. Overriding is a small change, but it means the code,
    not the model, decides the final status in that case.
  - Ask the model to re-check. A second call could resolve the contradiction.
    This costs more latency and quota, and I'd want it tested before relying on
    it.

  Do you want either of these, or is a visible warning enough for the final
  version?

❯ An independent review of steps 1 and 2 found weaknesses in the LLM selection
  evidence. Check whether these are real issue and fix them or documents them.

  ## Ground rules
  - Free-tier models only. Do not add paid providers or ask for paid keys.
  - FROZEN, do not modify: `SYSTEM_PROMPT` and `build_user_prompt` in
  `src/vcu_impact/prompt.py`, `eval/prompts/*`, and the LLM output models in
  `src/vcu_impact/models.py` (`LLMImpactResult`, `TestAssessment`,
  `ChangeInterpretation` and their enums). The schema sent to the model must
  stay byte-identical. If you believe one of these must change, stop and tell
  me instead of changing it.
  - Do not edit or delete anything under `eval/results/v1`, `v2` or `v3` that
  already exists. New runs add new files only.
  - Everything in English: code, comments, docs, commit messages. Small, clear
  commits.

  ## 1. Fix the S5 contradiction structurally (code only)
  Problem: with prompt v3, Groq qwen3.8-27b labels a test `directly_affected`
  even though its own `change_interpretations` says the change is
  `reworded_same_meaning` (S5, TV-005). The post-check currently only warns. In
  qwen's raw output, keys appear in alphabetical order, so `assessments` is
  generated before `change_interpretations`; this is probably why the model
  cannot use its own interpretation.

  Implement in `src/vcu_impact/analysis.py`:
  - If a test is `directly_affected` and ALL of its `related_requirements` have
  nature `reworded_same_meaning`, change its status to `not_affected` and keep
  a visible record. Do not touch tests with mixed related requirements, or
  with empty `related_requirements` (warn only for the latter).
  - The record of the change must NOT go into the LLM schema. Add it to the
  run-level output instead (for example a list of adjustments on `AnalysisRun`:
  test_id, original status, new status, reason), plus a warning string. The UI
  and report will need to show these later, so make them easy to consume.
  - Update tests in `tests/test_core.py`. The existing contradiction test
  asserts that the status is not changed; replace it with tests for: the
  adjustment applied, the adjustment recorded, mixed related requirements
  untouched, empty related requirements untouched, non-rewording changes
  untouched.
  - Make the scorer (`eval/evaluate.py`) able to report both numbers for
  existing runs: with the model's raw statuses, and with the new post-check
  applied to the stored raw output. Never mix them in one column; label them
  clearly, and write the post-check version of the summaries to new files (for
  example `summary_postcheck.*`), leaving the existing summaries untouched.
  - Wording matters: this is a safety net in the application, not an
  improvement of the model. The model still contradicts itself, and the app
  corrects it visibly. Say that in the docs.

  Optional, only if everything else is finished: test whether renaming the
  top-level field `assessments` so it sorts after `change_interpretations`
  changes qwen's S5 behaviour. This is a schema change, so do it on a branch,
  report it separately, and do not merge it without asking me.

  ## 2. Run prompt v3 on the other two models
  Run the current prompt (v3) on `gemini-3.5-flash` and `groq-gpt-oss-120b`
  using the existing resumable harness (`eval/evaluate.py run --only <name>`),
  same 7 scenarios and S1 three times. Results go to
  `eval/results/v3/<model>/`.
  - Skip `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` (it was unavailable).
  - Respect free-tier limits: runs are slow (Gemini 1–6 minutes each) and
  rate-limited. Run in the background, keep it resumable, and keep the existing
  retry and wait logic. If a model stays unavailable, record that as a result,
  not an error to hide.
  - Score with both views from item 1 (raw statuses, post-check applied).

  ## 3. Decide the decision rule BEFORE seeing results
  Write this into `docs/llm_evaluation.md` and commit it before running
  anything on the held-out scenarios: Groq qwen3.8-27b stays the primary model
  unless a challenger has strictly fewer critical failures (original 84 cells
  plus held-out cells, post-check applied) AND zero missed impacts. Ties go to
  qwen. Latency and free-tier friction are reported separately and weigh in the
  final human decision, not in this rule.

  ## 4. Add 3 held-out scenarios (written before any model sees them)
  Add scenarios S8 to S10 to `eval/dataset.yaml`, flagged as held-out, scored
  and reported SEPARATELY from the original 84 cells. Write the labels from the
  documents, with a `why` for each cell, and commit the dataset BEFORE running
  any model on them. Record the commit hash in the docs. Read the exact
  requirement texts and register contents yourself before writing the edits.
  Proposed scenarios (adapt if the documents contradict me):

  the time limit. Probes whether the model reasons about which stimulus levels

    not robustness.

    runs.

    (v3) because of S5 TV-005.

    results", but two cells got a post-hoc allowance.

  I documented all of this in the report, without changing any number.

  What changed

  - Rewording safety net. The post-check changes a directly_affected test to
    not_affected when every requirement it relates to was read as a rewording.
    - The change is recorded as an Adjustment on the run and as a warning,
      outside the LLM schema.
    - The docs call it a patch, not a better model, and name its risk: it trusts
      the unscored, unstable nature field.
    - The rule's main caveat is that it was written after seeing the S5 failure
      it fixes.
  - Scorer. It reports a raw view and a post-check view, in separate new files.
    The raw view reproduces all 7 existing summaries exactly.
  - Held-out scenarios. S8 to S10 were committed at 48d30d9 before any model ran
    on them. S10 has a 13-test register.
  - Decision rule. It was committed at 6a6abb8 before any new run.
  - Frozen files. SYSTEM_PROMPT, build_user_prompt, the output models and
    schema, eval/prompts/* and all existing results are unchanged. Git confirms
    it.

  the fallback, and gpt-oss-120b is dropped.
  Yes, you can keep it in a separate branch since it did not change the current

  Some caveats that need to be investigated:
  - The post-check is not validated. It was written after seeing the S5 failure
  and tested on S5, and it changed nothing on S8 to S10. So the 16/16 is
  in-sample. Claude Code says this in the docs, which is the right place for
  it.
  - The post-check has a real risk. It trusts nature, which is unscored and was
  unstable for S3. If the model wrongly calls a genuine change a rewording,
  the check turns a correctly flagged test into not_affected, which is a missed
  impact, the worst error type. A cheap guard fixes this: never downgrade when
  the deterministic diff found a numeric change in that requirement (the diff
  already computes numeric_changes). S5 has none, so the fix still works. It’s
  one small commit plus a test, and I recommend it.
  - The S8 TV-002 label is arguable, and Claude Code handled it correctly.
  Raising the trigger from 70% to 90% puts TV-002’s 85% pedal below it, so the
  test’s link to DR-01 goes stale. Labelling that “directly affected” is
  defensible, and both strong models did so. It’s the same
  relevance-versus-action blur as S4 TV-001. Don’t relabel after the fact,
  since the pre-registration is the point. Report it as a contested label, as
  Claude Code did, with the sensitivity showing the outcome doesn’t change.

  After investigating these limits, we will push everything. I updated the
  readme with the link to the conversation, after the application is running I
  will add the screenshots and then we can go to the assessment phase

  Your three caveats

  1. Post-check is unvalidated.
     - It was written after the S5 failure and tested on S5, so the 16/16 is
       in-sample, and it never fired on S8 to S10.
     - The docs already said so; I moved that into a stated limit in
       known_issues.md, the evaluation report and the README.
  2. Numeric guard (your suggestion).
     - The rewording rule now never fires when the deterministic diff found a
       changed number in a related requirement. It only warns, and the status
       stays as the model gave it.
     - Two new tests cover it. S5 has no numeric change, so every stored result
       is byte-identical.
     - It covers numeric changes only. A wrong "rewording" on a change without
       numbers (a negation, a changed condition word, an added or removed
       requirement) is still trusted, and the docs say so. I stopped at what you
       asked; extending it to added and removed requirements is a small change
       if you want it.
  3. S8 TV-002 label. I kept it and documented it as contested. Both strong
     models disagreed with it in the same way, and the outcome doesn't change if
     the label is relaxed.

  Field-order experiment (branch exp/field-order)

  It did not support the hypothesis.
  - Method: I renamed the JSON field so that change_interpretations is generated
    first. The key order changed in all 13 runs.
  - Result: qwen still marked S5 TV-005 directly_affected in 5 of 5 runs, while
    reading the change as reworded_same_meaning. So key order doesn't explain
    the contradiction, and the post-check stays.
  - Side effects: the change also moved the model's own status on 6 of 84 cells
    and made S1 outputs vary where they had been byte-identical. One run needed
    a repair retry.
  - Scope: it covered S1 to S7 only, with one model and no held-out scenarios.
    The write-up is docs/field_order_experiment.md on that branch.

  A scorer bug I found along the way

  While analysing the experiment I noticed my "raw" view was wrong for runs made
  after the post-check existed: it read the stored, already-corrected status,
  and I briefly misread S5 TV-005 as fixed. I fixed the scorer so the raw view
  recovers the model's own status by undoing the recorded corrections. No
  existing summary changed, and it's covered by a test.
